EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

US banks’ revenues remain resilient despite

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

### Page 3

3
Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

4
Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

10
Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?

III. The impact of cyberattacks

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

Marks & Spencer (Reported Apr 2025)
What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.
Equifax (Reported Sep 2017)
What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Notable cyber attacks: how they happened, estimated costs and share price reaction, cont.
Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating
the incident, it did not report any
operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

### Page 16

16
Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

### Page 17

17
Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.

dbDataInsights Consumer Survey Methodology
—Our proprietary survey conducted in 2026 (January – September) asked

consumers how they would respond if a company they shopped at regularly
was involved in a data breach or cyberattack, and how they would respond
once the disruption was resolved. This monthly survey covers households and
consumers in Germany, France, Italy, Spain, the United Kingdom, and the
United States.
—For each country referenced above, a nationally representative sample of more
than 500 households is surveyed each month (over 600 households in the US).

Collectively, this meant over the course of the survey (January – September
2026), we surveyed almost 30,000 households.

### Page 18

18
Material cyber incidents have contributed to negative credit outlooks
The infrastructure exists for cyberattacks to have a greater impact on credit quality should cyber disruption continue to accelerate
Source: S&P, Moody’s, Fitch, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Credit rating agencies consider both pre-breach resilience frameworks and
post-breach financial impacts. Agencies assess pre-breach cyber preparedness
through a lens of mitigating risk, while post-breach credit impacts centre on

financial capacity to withstand the resulting shock (considers scale and length of
the disruption); highly leveraged issuers with limited liquidity are seen as more
vulnerable. Negative outlook changes at JLR and Equifax illustrate the potential
credit impact. Fitch generally considers permanent reductions in cash generation
more credit-significant than temporary cash-flow delays.
—Ratings agencies are moving towards continuous monitoring. In 2021,

Moody’s invested $250mn in BitSight, which produces daily security ratings.
Moody’s has since built BitSight’s ratings into its credit tools, including Credit
Catalyst and EDF-X, for uses such as underwriting and ongoing monitoring of
borrowers and suppliers. This means lenders can see a company’s exposed
vulnerabilities as a potential indicator of corporate cyber risk.
S&P Global:
“The increasing frequency of cyberattacks and the potential for rapid deterioration in credit

profiles after an attack are risk factors relevant for our rating assessments.”
Moody’s:
“Cyberattacks can undermine the creditworthiness of companies, organizations and
governments, across all sectors and geographies, hitting revenues, increasing costs, and eroding
customer trust.”
Fitch Ratings:
“Although cyber events cannot be predicted with great accuracy, the associated risk can be
managed through proactive strategies, technology, policies, and vigilance.”

“The chief executive officer and board of directors hold ultimate responsibility for managing
cyber risk.”

Cyber governance & breaches can impact ESG scores
Sectors that hold the most personal data, and monetise it most directly, carry the greatest risk weighting in mainstream ESG scores
Source: MSCI ESG Ratings Methodology, Morningstar Sustainalytics ESG Risk Ratings, SASB Materiality Map, Deutsche Bank Resear ch.
Cybersecurity is generally seen as both a social and governance consideration
MSCI and Morningstar Sustainalytics both assess data privacy and security through management of the

risk, exposure to it and the controversies that follow a breach. Weak governance weighs on the score,
and a breach can cut it directly – more severe controversies draw larger deductions that persist for
longer.
Materiality varies sharply by sector. SASB flags data security as material across most consumer,
financial, healthcare and technology industries, with customer privacy concentrated in the most data-
intensive ones (see right).

### Page 20

IV. Investment, risk and opportunity

### Page 21

21
Europe’s dependency on US tech is firmly in the spotlight
Access to frontier AI models is now a security question; the funding gap for Europe will be hard to close
Source: Tikehau Capital – Tikehau Capital Barometer of European Investment in Cybersecurity – 7th edition 2026, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Growing cyber risk creates increased dependency on US cyber tech. Growing
concern over the cyber capabilities of frontier AI models has made ensuring

access to such technology a national security priority. US dominance over the AI
tech chain (e.g. chips, model development, etc), its willingness to impose AI-
related controls / restrictions, and previous pressure against Europe over tech
regulations has amplified concerns that dependence on US tech represents a
strategic threat to European sovereignty.
—Funding is the structural challenge. US cybersecurity start-ups attract almost 6x
the funding of European peers, and US-domiciled firms dominate the major

indices – 89% of the Nasdaq CTA Cybersecurity index and 93% of Global X
Cybersecurity ETF (which seeks to track the performance of Indxx Cybersecurity
Index) by weight. Europe has also seen home-grown firms such as Darktrace
acquired by US companies, though this reflects broader market trends rather
than anything cyber-specific. Ultimately, the US has a greater exit strategy for
start-ups as well.
—Boosting homegrown solutions a focus, but also a risk. EU policymakers are

seeking to address the dependency; e.g. the EU’s Cybersecurity and AI Action Plan
includes “scaling up Europe’s AI capabilities for cybersecurity” as one of its three
objectives. However, there are also risks associated with this endeavour; as one
dbDataInsights survey participant put it, “for most European organisations, the
bigger near-term danger would be moving too fast to less capable providers.”
—The wider relationship adds to the exposure. A more transactional

international environment, alongside Europe’s dependency on the US for energy
supplies and defence support, compounds the risk. Recent US-EU diesel
negotiations serve as a pertinent example of this, whereby the US reportedly
considered a diesel export ban to compel a release of European diesel reserves.

### Page 22

22
Cybersecurity as an opportunity
Tailwinds include demand related to keeping operations secure with AI acceleration and consolidation tailwinds
Source: dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research. Investors, defined as those who invest in stocks/ shares (either individually or through a broker/advisor) on a regular basis , were asked whether they perceived cybersecurity / data privacy as a risk or an opportunity for investors in 2026.
Broadly, DB’s company research view is that cybersecurity becomes even

more important in the AI era. Whereas cybersecurity has historically been viewed
as a blocker/drag on innovation and operational speed, it is now viewed as a
strategic enabler for secure AI adoption.
In particular, network security may see renewed growth after being viewed as a
more mature category. Rising AI usage should drive additional compute and data-
centre capacity, expanding the infrastructure requiring segmentation and
protection. Agentic workloads may also generate lower-throughput but more

frequent and persistent interactions across models, tools and data sources,
increasing the volume and complexity of network-level inspection.
Vendor consolidation has been a structural trend in cyber for some time, though not
necessarily linear. That said, the current environment may accelerate the
consolidation tailwinds as customers look to close security gaps that may arise from
a fragmented tool stack.
See further commentary from DB’s company research team here.

Our March 2026 “personal investor” survey showed a more positive view
among “ESG” investors. 58% in the US and 52% in the UK saw cybersecurity/data
privacy as an opportunity. Among all investors, US respondents favoured
opportunity over risk (46% vs 30%). UK views were more balanced (38% vs 40%).

### Page 24

24
Appendix A - Attack types
Top Attack types What it is How AI is helping attackers
Malware Software built to damage, disrupt or gain unauthorised access AI writes harmful software faster. Google has found malware that rewrites its own code while running to avoid detection, and criminal marketplaces selling AI malware-building tools.
Phishing Deceptive messages that trick people into giving up sensitive information Where AI’s effect is clearest: fluent, personalised lures without the old spelling and phrasing giveaways. Google says state-backed groups use AI to mass-produce them, and AI can fake voices and video.

Denial of service (DoS) Flooding a system so it cannot serve legitimate users Little reported evidence so far. AI can help find weak points and automate traffic floods, but the attack itself stays fairly simple.
Distributed denial of service
(DDoS)
The same attack launched from many
compromised devices at once
AI can accelerate the development and automation of attack tooling, while large-scale
botnets provide the distributed capacity behind hyper-volumetric DDoS attacks.

Man-in-the-middle (MitM) Intercepting or altering traffic between two parties Mainly indirect. AI-written phishing pages and deepfake voices make it easier to trick people into connecting through an attacker or approving fake sign-ins.
SQL injection Malicious SQL queries injected to manipulate backend database operations AI tools scan sites and spot flaws in database code faster, so less-skilled attackers can find weaknesses that once needed real expertise.

Cross-site scripting (XSS) Scripts injected into web pages to hijack user sessions As with SQL injection: AI speeds up finding vulnerable pages and writing the malicious code.
Zero-day exploits Attacking a software flaw before a patch is released A major new development. In May 2026 Google reported the first zero-day it believes was developed with AI. Its September 2026 report says the bigger effect is attackers misusing known flaws faster, before fixes land.

Advanced persistent threats
(APTs)
An intruder staying hidden in a network for long-
term
Google reports state-backed groups using AI across the whole attack, from target
research and lures to tooling and sifting stolen data.
Ransomware Encrypting data or locking device and demanding payment to release it NCSC expects AI to add to the global ransomware threat by lowering the skill needed to break in. AI also helps criminals find the most extortable data.

IoT exploitation Abusing weakly secured connected devices for entry AI can scan the internet for unpatched cameras, routers and storage devices and identify which flaws they carry.
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, National Cyber Security Centre, Google Threat Intelligence Group, Cloudflare, FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3)

Appendix B - European vs US cyber regulation
Europe US Federal
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Regulation Key requirements
NIS2 Directive In force 2023, transposition deadline Oct-24. Covers 18
sectors (up from seven) and creates an EU vulnerability
database. In-scope firms must manage cyber and
supply-chain risk and notify authorities of significant
incidents. Management bodies must approve and
oversee cyber risk measures, with potential liability for

infringements.
Cybersecurity Act In force 2019. Establishes an EU-wide certification
framework for ICT products, services, processes and
managed security services, and made the EU
cybersecurity body ENISA permanent.
Cyber Resilience Act In force 2024, main obligations from 2027.
Manufacturers and retailers of products with digital
elements must deliver security by design, a defined
support period and security fixes within it, plus
mandatory vulnerability management and disclosure.

Digital Operational
Resilience Act (DORA)
In force 2023, applies from 2025. Financial-services
specific, covering own-firm and third-party risk. Requires
an ICT business continuity policy, regular resilience
testing and disclosure of major incidents, and gives
regulators oversight of critical ICT providers.
Cyber Solidarity Act In force 2025. Creates a European Cybersecurity Alert
System to detect threats and share cross-border
warnings, plus a Cyber Emergency Mechanism to boost

preparedness and response.
Regulation Key requirements
White House Accord on
Super Intelligence
(2026)
Emphasises self-regulation to ensure AI safety, with a call for
external evaluations and the possibility of formal regulation in
future. Not legally binding; signed by President Trump and
leading AI companies.
Executive Order on
Cybersecurity (2025)
Highlights risk from foreign actors and orders further action
on defending digital infrastructure, securing the most vital

and governance.
CIRCIA (passed 2022,
not yet in effect)
In-scope entities, focused on critical infrastructure, must
report covered incidents to CISA within 72 hours and
ransomware payments within 24 hours of payment.
Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act
(amended 2024)
Financial institutions must explain information-sharing
practices and safeguard customer data, running a security
programme covering risk assessment, service-provider
oversight and incident response.

Federal Information
Security Modernization
Act (2014)
Applies to federal agencies and contractors: system security
plans, continuous monitoring, incident response capability
and mandatory incident reporting.
Federal Acquisition
Regulation (cyber
provisions 2016)
Governs federal procurement, setting baseline cyber
requirements for suppliers so cyber risk is built into
government contracting. Agencies may impose stricter
provisions.

《政治與政策打擊空頭，日圓匯率貶值壓力大減》
日本銀行於9月18日舉行利率政策決議會議，決議將
政策利率由1.0%調升一碼至1.25%，符合市場預期。
日銀聲明稿表示，即便目前日本CPI仍低於2%目標，
但考量中東地緣衝突升溫、全球AI投資熱潮以及日圓
貶值壓力，通膨上行風險有所升高，且企業成本上升
持續轉嫁至消費者，因此升息一碼。
不過，日銀仍強調目前金融環境整體偏寬鬆，企業融
資與銀行放貸功能運作良好，因此即使利率上升，短
期對經濟活動衝擊有限。並表示，未來政策路徑將取
決於薪資成長、核心通膨及景氣表現，若AI相關投資
持續帶動出口與設備投資擴張，加上日圓走勢及能源
價格進一步推升物價，則不排除利率進一步升息。
而鑑於日銀被迫加快升息腳步，以及財政可持續性疑

慮在高市內閣推動大規模財政政策之下短期難解，加
以第四季日本2027財年預算案決議 ，預期支出續創
歷史新高的情況下，預估日債殖利率維持高檔；待到
明年2Q27聯準會轉向降息預期逐漸升高，以及日本
升息終點接近方有較明顯的回落空間。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理
5
貨幣政策正常化以及財政疑慮推升日債殖利率走高
• 日銀升息主因在於通膨風險與
匯率壓力，搭配財政擴張預期，
使市場對日債供給及財政永續
性的擔憂升溫，殖利率仍易維
持高檔。

《日本年內再升息取決地緣局勢發展》
展望未來，日銀今年已分別在 6、9月各升息一碼、
合計兩碼至 1.25%，已正式進入日銀預估中性利率
1.1%~2.5%區間下緣；另一方面 ，日銀預估今年經
濟成長率0.6%，僅略高於內閣府公布的潛在經濟成
長率的0.5%，加以目前最新8月份CPI僅1.9%，低於
預期以及2.0%目標外，核心CPI更僅有1.7%。
如根據標準泰勒法則：政策利率 = 中性利率 + 𝟎. 𝟓 ∗
(通膨缺口) + 𝟎. 𝟓 ∗ (產出缺口)
目前日本通膨缺口-0.1、產出缺口+0.1的情況下，理
論的政策利率恰巧等於中性利率，而考量1.25%已在
中性利率區間 ，且利率已創31年新高，為避免經濟
與通膨轉向下行，本行認為年內不宜再度升息。

然而，日銀9月份的升息除了考量地緣導致通膨上行
風險升高下，另一方面也是受到日圓貶值壓力加大，
以及回應美國財政部持續對貨幣政策加快緊縮的期
待，因此如中東地緣衝突持續難以降溫，布油長時間
維持百元高位，抑或日圓匯率再度明顯上破160美元
大關，日本央行仍有在12月會期再度升息的空間。
重點摘要
5
• 依通膨與產出缺口推估，目前
政策利率已接近合理區間，除
非油價再度大漲或日圓大幅走
弱，否則年底再升息必要性有
限。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理
日本CPI連續8個月低於2.0%目標

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
《利匯率短期走弱不易，日股表現相對波動》
如前所述，在地緣升溫以及美國政治壓力之下，日銀
9月提前升息一碼至1.25%，同時市場預期12月份仍
有升息空間，鑒於日央被迫加快升息腳步，以及財政
可持續性疑慮在高市推動大規模財政政策之下短期難
解，預期日債殖利率維持高檔。
日圓匯率方面，7月底美日聯合干預後，加以美國財
政部長貝森特持續高調警告市場勿做空日圓，使日圓
自164兌一美元的近年低位回升，而根據商品期貨交
易委員會(CFTC)資料顯示，目前日圓期貨部位已由淨
空頭轉向明顯的淨多頭，短線日圓回貶空間有限。
基此，日本利率與匯率回落的空間不大，而對日本企
業而言，日圓升值將壓縮出口商獲利及海外收益換算
效益；此外，利率上升也將提高企業融資成本與股票

估值折現率。因此短線日股評價擴張空間有限，加以
下半年企業獲利雖維持成長，但動能較上半年明顯放
緩的情況下，本行短線下調對日股看法至中立。
不過，中長期經濟基本面以及企業獲利預期仍舊正
向，日本政府改革紅利延續，待預算案以及隨著明年
通膨預期降溫後，利匯率壓力趨緩市場重回基本面主
導格局下，日股中長期多頭趨勢還未改變。
重點摘要
5
• 升息與匯率升值壓縮企業獲利
及評價空間，使日股短期表現
承壓。但若未來利率與通膨壓
力緩和，市場焦點可望重新回
歸企業基本面。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行
彙整

《盤勢觀點-利匯率壓力推動日股資金輪動加快》
綜上導致日本股市下半年投資風格輪動加速，上半年
大漲的AI半導體與科技成長股表現相對疲弱；資金則
流向反映市場對貨幣政策正常化、日圓升值帶動內需
及評價面優勢類股，包含金融(升息)、航運(地緣)、
商社娛樂(升值、內需)等價值與景氣循環族群。
從個股表現觀察，漲幅前段班以能源航運類、金融及
內需消費相關企業主 。其中，山葉發動機 、Recruit
Holdings、日本郵船 、樂天娛樂及萬代南夢宮漲幅
均超過40%；其次，銀行類股表現同樣優於大盤，且
內需為主的中小銀行，如青空銀行、靜岡金融集團、
等漲幅皆超過 20%，標線優於以外需為主的三大銀
行，顯示市場持續押注日本央行升息所帶來的利差改

善效益及升值對於內需消費動能的提振利多。
相比之下上半年表現亮眼的AI與半導體成長型類股，
受利匯率走升明顯承壓。其中，下半年東京威力科創
下跌、LASERTEC、DISCO、SCREEN Holdings、瑞
薩電子下跌皆超過20%；此外，半導體材料與零組件
族群亦同步受抑，包括信越化學、SUMCO 、東京應
化也都有雙位數跌幅。
重點摘要
5
• 下半年資金自高評價AI及半導

股，反映市場預期升息及日圓

展望未來1~2個季度，在日本央行升息預期及債務疑
慮難以降溫，利率與匯率維持高位機率較高；但於此
同時，經濟基本面仍具有韌性，就業市場維持緊張、
企業治理改革持續推進，內需復甦延續下，金融與價
值型類股仍有望維持相對優勢。而先前累積較高漲幅
與評價的AI供應鏈，在修正之後雖有布局空間，但表
現仍會震盪，仍需觀察獲利成長動能的延續性。
因此，即便短線對日股轉中性看待，但預期三大主軸
仍可有優於大盤的表現。首先，金融股受惠升息以及
內續穩健，隨著日本通膨維持相對高位，市場預期日
本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化，帶動銀行淨利差改
善，地方銀行表現優於大型銀行。其次，商社與資源
循環產業，包括伊藤忠商事、豐田通商、三菱商事及
住友商事等綜合商社，受惠於資源價格、企業治理改

革及高股東回報政策，持續吸引資金流入。第三，內
需與娛樂產業，包括任天堂、萬代南夢宮等公司，受
內需穩健以及全球數位娛樂市場成長。
具體而言，日股配置上建議適度提高金融、商社與內
需類股比重；而半導體族群獲利仍舊明確，待年末或
1Q27利匯率壓力趨緩後，留意資金回流行情。

《盤勢觀點-能源推升澳洲再升息，股市壓力加大》
隨著地緣衝突再升溫、布油上破百元，澳洲央行跟隨
歐美日腳步 ，在9月會期再度升息至 4.6%，創下
2011年以來的政策利率新高 。聲明稿表示，中東衝
突擴大是本次升息的首要因素，政策邏輯和聯準會風
險管理式升息相近，而非已見到通膨失控的風險。
政策前瞻方面，本次聲明稿中雖表示不排除進一步升
息的空間，但對經濟環境採取了「正反雙向表述」，
顯示澳央並不對下次升息預設立場，文中表示企業投
資、AI相關資本支出及外部需求仍為經濟提供支撐，
但另一方面升息環境與高能源價格正持續對經濟形成
壓力，消費支出已逐步放緩 ，多數主要城市房價下
跌，8月份失業率卻由 4.48%升至4.65%、高於市場

預期且細項結構不佳，因此年內是否再升息，本行預
期將取決於中東地緣以及國際油價發展。
綜上，持續升息後澳洲基本面降溫趨勢明顯，但通膨
壓力有迫使央行貨幣政策難以轉向寬鬆，加以企業獲
利未見改善動能，澳洲股市難以樂觀維持中性展望。
另外留意，澳幣期貨部位近期轉向淨空頭，顯示澳幣
短線接近滿足點，也已接近本行目標價。
重點摘要
• 澳洲面臨油價推升通膨與景
氣放緩並存的局面 ，高利率
將持續抑制經濟活動 ，在企
業獲利未改善前 ，澳股仍以
中性看待較為合理。

[オーストラリア]～エネルギー価格の上昇でインフレ率は加速するも､コアインフレ率は横ばいで推移～ 
30 日に発表された８月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋4.0％となり、 前月 （同＋3.5％） から加速し、
13 ヵ月連続でＲＢＡ （オーストラリア準備銀行） が定める目標レンジ （２～３％） を上回る伸びと
なった。前月比は＋0.42％と前月（同＋1.02％）から上昇ペースは鈍化したものの、上昇が続いて
いる。 食料品価格の上昇に一服感が出ている一方、 原油価格の高止まりを反映してエネルギー価格
は上昇するなど、生活必需品の物価はまちまちの動きをみせている。オーストラリアでは、トリム
平均値（刈り込み平均値）ベースのインフレ率をコアインフレ率としており、８月は前年同月比＋
3.6％と３ヵ月連続で同じ伸びとなり、11 ヵ月連続で目標域を上回った。 ただし、 前月比は＋0.24％
と前月（同＋0.48％）から上昇ペースが鈍化し、６ヵ月ぶりの低水準となるなど、インフレ圧力が
緩やかに後退している様子がうかがえる。なお、ＲＢＡは月次の物価統計のうち、物価変動の大き
い財と観光を除いたベースを注視してきた。８月は前年同月比＋3.9％と前月（同＋3.8％）からわ
ずかに伸びが加速し、14 ヵ月連続で目標域を上回った。 とはいえ、 前月比は＋0.19％と前月 （同＋

### Page 2

2/4 
0.79％）から上昇ペースが鈍化している。輸送コストが上昇する一方、金融市場における豪ドル相
場の上昇は輸入インフレ圧力を後退させており、 財価格は落ち着いた動きをみせている。 サービス
物価も比較的落ち着いた動きとなるなど、インフレ圧力が後退する兆しがうかがえる。

[韓国]～世界的な AI・半導体需要の旺盛さが輸出をけん引､単月ベースの輸出額は過去最高に～ 
30 日に発表された８月の鉱工業生産は前年同月比▲2.2％となり、 前月 （同＋4.0％） から３ヵ月
ぶりの減少に転じた。前月比も▲4.8％と前月（同＋0.5％）から３ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じており、
これまで続いてきた持ち直しの動きに変化の兆しが出ている。 分野別では、 鉱業や建設業の生産は
拡大している一方、 製造業や公益関連の生産は減少しており、 分野ごとのばらつきが鮮明になって
いる。製造業では、縫製品や木製品など軽工業関連で生産拡大の動きがみられるものの、主力の輸
出財である半導体など電子部品関連で生産拡大の動きに一服感が出ているほか、 電気機械関連の生
産も減少している。 さらに、 大手自動車メーカーでのストライキの影響で自動車の生産が大きく下
振れするなど、幅広い輸出関連産業で減産の動きが出たことが全体を下押しした。 
１日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋83.5％となり、前月（同＋68.7％）から伸びが加
速した。 当研究所が試算した季節調整値に基づく前月比も２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 単月ベース
の輸出額は過去最高を更新した。 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体需要の旺盛さを反映して半導体
の輸出が拡大するとともに、 コンピュータをはじめとする電気機械関連の輸出も堅調に推移してい
る。また、原油価格の高止まりを反映して石油製品関連の輸出額も押し上げられている。国・地域
別では、最大の輸出先である中国向けのほか、米国向けも旺盛な推移をみせている。一方、輸入額
は前年同月比＋26.0％となり、前月（同＋22.4％）から伸びが加速した。前月比も３ヵ月ぶりの拡
大に転じており、輸出の堅調さを反映して輸入も持ち直しの動きを強めている。財別では、輸出の
旺盛な動きを反映して素材・部材関連の輸入が堅調に推移しているほか、 原油をはじめとする商品
市況の高止まりも輸入額を押し上げている。 その結果、 貿易収支は＋498.52 億ドルと前月 （＋347.85
億ドル）から黒字幅が拡大し、単月ベースの黒字幅は過去最高となった。 
２日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.9％となり、 ７ヵ月連続で中銀目標 （２％）
を上回ったものの、 前月 （同＋3.1％） から伸びは鈍化した。 ただし、 前月比は＋0.32％と前月 （同
＋0.23％） から上昇ペースが加速している。 異常気象の影響により生鮮品を中心とする食料品価格
が上昇しているほか、 原油価格の高止まりを反映してエネルギー価格も上昇するなど、 生活必需品

を中心にインフレ圧力が強まっていることが影響している。 なお、 食料品とエネルギーを除いたコ

[インドネシア]～食料品とエネルギーの物価上昇を反映して､インフレ率は再び加速している～ 
１日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋3.28％となり、前月（同＋3.19％）から加速
したものの、 引き続き中銀の目標レンジ （2.5±１％） 内で推移している。 前月比も＋0.30％と前月
（同＋0.21％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、 生鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格が上昇している
ほか、 原油価格の高止まりを反映してエネルギー価格も上昇するなど、 生活必需品を中心にインフ
レ圧力が強まっている。なお、 食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋2.84％
となり、前月（同＋2.92％）から伸びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.10％と前月（同＋0.21％）から上
昇ペースが鈍化している。 輸送コストの上昇圧力に加え、 金融市場におけるルピア安を背景とする
輸入インフレ懸念がくすぶるものの、幅広く財価格は落ち着いた推移をみせている。一方、サービ
ス物価は上昇しており、物価の動きにはばらつきがみられる。 
また、同日に発表された８月の輸出額は前年同月比＋6.7％となり、前月（同＋6.1％）からわず
かに伸びが加速した。 ただし、 当研究所が試算した季節調整値に基づく前月比は２ヵ月連続で減少

Document 513: 为什么值得专程去一趟香港金融科技周？答案不只在会场

Source PDF: all report pdf/为什么值得专程去一趟香港金融科技周？答案不只在会场.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

作为连接资本、创新与全球市场的 “ 超级联系⼈ ” ，⾹港对于投资者和创业者，正在孕育哪些新机
遇 ? AI 正如何从独⽴的技术议题，变成所有产业都要具备的新能⼒？中国企业出海，如何跨越监
管、规则与⽂化的壁垒，真正接⼊国际资本、客⼾与产业伙伴体系？
11 ⽉，我们⼀起去⾹港和深圳，近距离找答案。
11 ⽉ 2 ⽇⾄ 3 ⽇，⾛进⾹港⾦融科技周，听全球⾦融机构、科技企业和监管者的观点分享；
11 ⽉ 4 ⽇，与⾦融机构⾯对⾯，交流资产配置、财富管理热点；
11 ⽉ 5 ⽇，⾛访深圳企业，看 AI 、⽀付、⾃动驾驶和机器⼈正在如何落地。
为什么值得专⻔去⼀趟？
这不是⼀次单纯的⼤会观摩。我们把两天国际会议、⼀天⾦融机构交流、⼀天企业参访放在同
⼀趟⾏程⾥：先听全球视⻆，再看⾦融实务，最后到企业⼀线。
第⼀站｜ 11 ⽉ 2—3 ⽇：在 2026 ⾹港⾦融科技周，看⻅全球⾦融与科技的未
来
承接逾⼗载影响⼒，⾹港⾦融科技周已成为极具影响⼒的年度旗舰盛会。今年⼤会以 “AI+ 、⾦融
+ 、出海 ” 为三⼤引擎，设置 “ 政策与前瞻 ”“AI 及前沿科技 ”“ 数字资产及 Web3”“ ⾦融 AI”“ 全球科创 · 智

汇中国 ” 等⼗⼤论坛。
嘉宾阵容中，既有⾹港特区⾏政⻓官李家超、财政司司⻓陈茂波等政府代表，也有汇丰集团⾏
政总裁 Georges Elhedery 、富兰克林邓普顿 CEO Jenny Johnson ，以及腾讯公司副总裁、腾讯
⾦融科技业务负责⼈林海峰等业界⼈⼠。
届时，预计将有 45000+ 全球参与者、 1000+ 演讲嘉宾和 800+ 赞助商、参展商及初创企业⻬聚⾹
港。对关注创新科技、全球市场与跨境资产配置的读者，这两天值得从三个⻆度深⼊了解。我
们将重点参加 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇⾄ 3 ⽇的主论坛和展览。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

我们建议带着三个问题去听：
AI 在⾦融机构⾥究竟⽤到哪⼀步？从投研到客⼾服务，哪些场景已经产⽣可衡量的价值？
⾹港的数字资产⾏业最新进展如何？有哪些值得关注的趋势和动向？
中国企业出海，⾦融机构能提供什么？跨境⽀付、贸易融资、财富管理等需求将如何变化？
第⼆站｜ 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇：⾛进⾹港⾦融机构，把投资话题聊具体
在⾹港，我们安排的不是泛泛的⾏业介绍，⽽是⼏组投资者经常关⼼、却很难在公开会议上聊
透的话题。
某国际⼤⾏：围绕全球资产配置、 AI 产业变化、中美权益市场等，交流机构的市场观察。
拔萃资本：与机构业务总监于锦交流家族及⾼净值客⼾财富管理，了解服务模式与资产配置
思路。
银乾国际：了解⾹港证监会第 9 类受规管活动（资产管理）牌照的申请、运营及相关业务实
践。
同⼀天，从市场观点聊到财富管理，再聊到资管牌照，让 “ 跨境资产配置 ” 从概念落到更具体的产
品和业务实践。晚上，我们还安排在跑⻢地观看赛⻢、共进晚餐，让同⾏者有机会继续交流。
第三站｜ 11 ⽉ 5 ⽇：去深圳，看技术如何变成产品和⽣意

⾹港讨论资本、政策和国际市场；深圳则更适合看技术如何进⼊真实业务。有三条企业路线，
覆盖⾦融科技、互联⽹、⾃动驾驶、 AI 和机器⼈。
路线⼀：微众银⾏、字节跳动、⼩⻢智⾏。

### Page 3

路线⼆：普渡机器⼈、财付通、云天励⻜。
路线三：中国平安、逐际动⼒、众擎机器⼈。
每位参与者将随机分配⾄其中⼀条路线，参访三家企业；实际接待企业和交流内容以最终安排
为准。现场重点不只是看展⽰，⽽是理解产品⾯向谁、解决什么问题，以及商业化推进到了哪
⼀步。
谁适合参加？
如果你持续关注 AI 与科技投资、全球⾦融市场、数字资产、财富管理或跨境资产配置，希望把公
开信息与机构观点、企业⼀线观察相互印证，这趟⾏程会更有针对性。
⾏程速览
11 ⽉ 1 ⽇：抵达⾹港，具体集合安排以⾏前通知为准。
11 ⽉ 2—3 ⽇：参加⾹港⾦融科技周主论坛、展览及相关分享。
11 ⽉ 4 ⽇：⾹港⾦融机构交流；晚间跑⻢地观赛及晚餐。
11 ⽉ 5 ⽇：深圳企业参访（三条路线随机分配）。
报名咨询｜限额 25 ⼈
华尔街⻅闻本次港深深度调研⾏限额 25 ⼈。想了解参会安排、调研权益、费⽤及报名⽅式，请
扫码添加 Alpha ⼩助⼿咨询。企业接待、分享嘉宾与具体⽇程以最终确认信息为准。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

冠德 (2520 TT)

維持評等  
增加持股(Overweight) 
2H26 開始陸續推案 ，展望 配息仍 具高殖利率 且評價仍低  
⚫ 3Q26 陸續推出 3 個建案合計總銷 高達百億  
⚫ AI 與科 技產業挹注商辦市場成長  
⚫ 預估 冠德 2026~2027 年度配息仍可 望 具高殖利率  
冠德 高毛利個案貢獻獲利成長 ： 冠德 1H26 合併營收 132.26 億元 ，
YoY+31.39 %，毛利率 26.22 %，較去年同期增加 2.48 個百分點，其中
2Q 單季毛利率提升至 27.81% ，主因來自於高單價及高毛利成屋個案
「冠德信義」的貢獻， 1H26 單季稅後歸屬母公司獲利 11.1 億元 ，較
去年同期成長 61.48% ，1H26 EPS 約 1.82 元；累計 01~0 9/2026 合併營
收 189.4 億元，較去年同期成長 16.94 %。 
2026 年下半年推案量 119 億元 ： 冠德預計 2026 年下半年 推出 3 個建
案，包括「台中裕毛屋案」位於台中市北屯區 ，規劃樓層 24F/B4F ，
總銷金額 67 億元， 目前 已取得使用執照， 10/2028 公開銷售 ； 「台中
G5 案」 位於台中市北屯區 ，規劃樓層 22F/B5F ，總銷金額約 16 億元，
已取得使用執照，預計 11/2026 公開銷售； 「直興案」位於台北市萬華
區，規劃樓層 21F/B5F ，總銷金額 36 億元， 10/2026 公開銷售，今年
台北及台中地區 3 個推案 合計總銷高達 119 億元。  
儲備個案量能充足： 目前冠 德 9 個儲備個案 基地面積總 計 2.23 萬 坪，
合計總銷 802 億元 ，住宅佔 66.79%、商辦佔 18.23% 及住商佔 14.98% ，
主要百億級個案近況方面， 「台電南港案」總銷 130 億元 ，目前已開
工，預計 3Q28 完工；「行 二行三 案」位於北市中正區， 總銷 221 億元，
10/2026 都市更新核定，預計 2Q27 開工；「G9-1 案」位於台中西屯區，
總銷 110 億元， 07/2025 建照審查掛件，預計 2Q27 開工；「E1E2 案」
於 10/2023 都市更新報核，預計 4Q28 開工；「專 45 案」總銷 120 億
元，12/2025 簽約， 4Q26 都更報核 ，預計 3Q28 開工。  
預期冠德配息 仍具 高殖利率 ：冠德 建案銷售率持續推展，規劃下半年
推案量能仍有百億元，目前在手 儲備個案預計總銷金額 802 億元；投
顧上調 冠德 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 至 3.95/3.9 9 元，預估至 202 7 年底
每股淨值約 43. 44 元，目前 PBR 約 0.85 倍，近 1 年 PBR 平均約 0.89
倍，上調 配息 展望至 1.80~1.8 5 元，殖利率約 近 5%仍具高殖利率，目
標價 43 元( 202 7 年 PBR 1.0 倍)。 
風險因子 ：台灣房市景氣轉弱 ，推案銷售與完工進度遞延 。

買進 Buy (B) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力極具吸引力 
增加持股 Overweight (OW) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力略有吸引力

### Page 1

十月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  印刷電路板 ‧ 台灣

臻鼎-KY 
(4958.TW/4958 TT)

ABF 營收成長超前與 HDI 擴產加碼，2027 獲利上調 
重要訊息 
9 月營收達 263.6 億元，月增 27.5%、年增 36.1%；3Q26 營收 646.2 億
元，季增 33.4%、年增 36.4%，達成我們及市場 3Q26 預估之 98.8%、
101，符合預期。  
評論及分析  
ABF 載板營收進度超前，上調 2026-27 年佔比至 5.7%、10.3%。3Q26 在
漲價效應下與新產能開出，ABF 營收貢獻預估可望超越 BT，載板營收佔比
上調至 12%； 2026-27 年載板佔比上修至 11.2%、16.9%(原 9.6%、
13.6%)，ABF 營收預期開始超越 BT。 
加碼擴產中高階 HDI，HDI 伺服器增速大幅超越 HLC 伺服器。2027 年成長
重心偏向高階 HDI Server， 管理層將 2030 年前新廠數增加至 31 座，總廠
房數達 59 座，目前已開工廠房 22 座(11 座為 HDI 廠、7 座為載板)，其中
7 座已開始裝機，2 027 年開始試產到量產。  
載板漲價效應及切入 AWS T4 及 Google V9 伺服器，2027  AI 應用佔比上
調。我們預估在載板漲價效應 使營收進度優預期下， 3Q26 AI 應用(伺服器、
光模塊、載板 )佔比達 26%，目前光模塊營收進度維持 2027 倍增不變，取
決泰國、淮安新廠實際貢獻時間；AI 伺服器預估在輝達 (美) VR computing 
tray 目標 20% 以上市占率，而則在 AWS(美) T4、Google (美) V9 主板將切
入，綜上我們將 26-27 AI 應用佔比上修至 25%、42%。 
投資建議  
由於新廠產能集中 2H26 開出，可認列的營收與獲利有限， 2027 年則由新
廠完全貢獻、高階 HDI 放量及產品組合改善 下，帶動營收與獲利提升。 我們
上調 2027 獲利 13%，維持「增加持股」評等，目標價上調至 775 元，相
當於 2027 每股盈餘預估的 26 倍本益比。  
投資風險  
美系客戶智慧型手機銷售不如預期，競爭者技術追趕超越預期。  
增加持股‧維持

速大幅超越傳統  HLC 伺服器 
3. 載板漲價及切入 AWS T4 及 Google 
V9 伺服器， 2026-27 AI 應用佔比上
調。

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 應用比重 
臻鼎科技設立於 2006 年 6 月，為台灣第一大軟板供應商，主
要產品為硬式印刷電路板 (R-PCB)、軟性印刷電路板 (FPC)、高
密度連接板(HDI)與 IC 載板(IC Substrate)  、類載板(SLP)及軟硬
結合板及 COF，2025 年合併營收年增 6%達 1,825 億元，
2024 年營收應用佔比為行動通訊 64.2%、電腦消費 25.9%、
IC 載板 5.2%、汽車雷達 /基地台/伺服器/其他 4.7%。

台灣 臻鼎-KY

十月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

Document 625: 実質賃金はプラス継続も、先行きは再び下振れリスク（26年8月毎月勤労統計） ～所定内給与は着実に増加で賃金の基調はなお良好。_363bc399

Source PDF: all report pdf/実質賃金はプラス継続も、先行きは再び下振れリスク（26年8月毎月勤労統計） ～所定内給与は着実に増加で賃金の基調はなお良好。_363bc399.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 10 月 7 日

1/5 
実質賃金はプラス継続も、先行きは再び下振れリスク（26 年 8 月毎月勤労統計） 
～所定内給与は着実に増加で賃金の基調はなお良好。物価上昇が懸念材料～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年８月の毎月勤労統計では、 現金給与総額が前年比＋3.8％、 実質賃金が同＋1.5％とな
った。７月からは伸びが鈍化したものの、依然として高い伸び。共通事業所ベースでも現金
給与総額は同＋2.8％と増加、実質賃金も同＋0.6％程度とプラスを維持している。所得環境
の改善は続いており、良好な結果と判断してよい。 
○ 共通事業所ベースの現金給与総額の伸びが前月から小幅に鈍化した主因は、特別給与が減少
したこと。ボーナス支給月以外の特別給与は月ごとの振れが大きいため、これをネガティブ
に受け止める必要はない。 
○ 共通事業所ベースの所定内給与は前年比＋3.1％と、７月に続いて＋３％台の高い伸びを維持
した。一般労働者は同＋2.8％、パートタイム労働者は同＋4.9％。春闘賃上げの給与への反
映が概ね出揃う時期に入っても高い伸びが続いており、賃金の基調は引き続き良好である。
先行きも前年比＋２％台後半から＋３％強程度の伸びが続く可能性が高い。 
○ 名目賃金は今後も高めの伸びを維持するとみられる一方、年末にかけて物価上昇率は高まる
可能性が高く、実質賃金が一時的にマイナスに転じることも十分考えられる。企業部門が好
調さを維持するなか、家計部門が物価上昇の逆風にどこまで耐えられるかが、景気の持続性
を左右する重要なポイントになる。 
○ 次に注目されるのが2027 年春闘である。 人手不足や物価上昇、 高水準の企業収益が賃上げを
後押しする一方、人件費負担やコスト上昇は下押し要因となる。今後、連合や経団連などの
動きを通じて、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢がどの程度維持されるかを確認していくことになる。

1. 名目賃金、実質賃金とも増加で良好な内容 
厚生労働省から公表された2026 年８月の毎月勤労統計は、名目賃金、実質賃金ともに増加し、
総じて良好な結果となった。所定内給与が高い伸びを維持するなか、実質賃金もプラス圏が続い
ており、所得環境の改善が続いている。 
現金給与総額は前年比＋3.8％となった。７月の同＋4.3％からは伸びが鈍化したものの、引き

### Page 2

2/5 
続き高い伸びである。内訳では、所定内給与が同＋3.8％と高い伸びを維持していることが牽引役
となった。一般労働者の所定内給与は同＋3.8％、パートタイム労働者も同＋4.1％となってお
り、幅広い層で賃金増加が続いている。また、名目賃金から物価変動の影響を除いた実質賃金も
前年比＋1.5％となった。実質賃金は2026 年入り後、プラス基調で推移しており、家計の所得環
境改善が続いている。 
なお、報道等で言及されることの多いこの本系列は、調査対象事業所の部分入れ替えやベンチ
マーク更新等の影響を受けやすく、月次の賃金動向の基調を判断するうえでは必ずしも適さな
い。そうした観点からは、１年前と当月の双方で回答している調査対象のみに限定して集計した
「共通事業所」ベースの前年比を併せて確認することが望ましい。 
この共通事業所ベースでみると、８月の現金給与総額は前年比＋2.8％となった。一般労働者は
同＋2.5％、パートタイム労働者は同＋5.1％である。本系列の＋3.8％と比べれば伸びは低いもの
の、共通事業所ベースでも３％近い伸びが続いており、賃金の基調は引き続き良好である。 
なお、共通事業所ベースの現金給与総額の伸びは７月の同＋2.9％からやや鈍化したが、これを
賃金の基調悪化とみる必要はない。主因は特別給与であり、７月の前年比＋2.6％から８月は同
▲7.9％へ低下した。ボーナス支給月以外の特別給与は月ごとの振れが大きいため、この動きを重
視する必要はないだろう。むしろ、基調を示す所定内給与は７月、８月ともに同＋3.1％と高い伸
びを維持している。現金給与総額の伸びはやや鈍化したものの、その中身をみれば賃金の基調は
しっかりしていると評価できる。 
なお、共通事業所ベースの現金給与総額を「持家の帰属家賃を除く総合」で実質化すると、８
月の実質賃金は前年比＋0.6％程度となる。本系列の同＋1.5％ほどの強さではないものの、共通
事業所ベースでも実質賃金はプラス圏を維持している。本系列、共通事業所のいずれでみても、
名目賃金が物価上昇を上回る状況が続いており、所得環境の改善を改めて確認する内容だったと
評価できる。

2. 所定内給与は 3％台の高い伸びを維持 
所定内給与（共通事業所ベース、以下同じ）は前年比＋3.1％と、７月と同じ高い伸びを維持し
た。これで３ヵ月連続の＋３％台となる。内訳では、一般労働者が同＋2.8％、パートタイム労働
者が同＋4.9％となった。一般労働者が＋２％台後半のしっかりした伸びを維持する一方、パート
タイム労働者も＋５％近い高い伸びとなっており、賃金の基調は引き続き良好である。 
所定内給与増加の背景にあるのが、2026 年春闘で実現した高い賃上げである。連合の春闘最終
集計では、賃上げ率が5.01％となった。2025 年春闘からはやや鈍化したものの、それでも５％台
の高い賃上げが実現した。また、300 人未満の組合でも4.69％と、前年の4.65％を小幅に上回っ
ており、中小企業でも高い賃上げが実施されている。 
春闘賃上げの給与への反映は７～８月頃までで概ね出揃う。そうしたなか、８月の所定内給与
が前年比＋3.1％と３％台の伸びを維持したことは、2026 年春闘で実現した高い賃上げが実際の給
与にも着実に反映されていることを示している。 
また、このところ強いのがパートタイム労働者であり、パートの賃金上昇が所定内給与全体を
下支えしている。最低賃金引き上げの効果に加え、深刻な人手不足を背景に、企業が人材確保の
ため時給を引き上げていることが影響しているとみられる。 
このように、８月の所定内給与は、春闘賃上げの反映が概ね出揃う時期に入っても＋３％台の
高い伸びを維持しており、賃金の基調は引き続き良好である。人手不足を背景とした賃上げ圧力
も根強く、所定内給与は先行きも前年比＋２％台後半から＋３％強程度の高い伸びを維持する可
能性が高いだろう。

3.  物価上昇で実質賃金は再びマイナス転化の可能性も 
このように、名目賃金は先行きも高めの伸びを維持するとみられる。もっとも、実質賃金の改
善がこのまま続くかどうかは、物価動向に大きく左右される。2026 年末～2027 年初にかけては、
物価上昇による実質賃金の下振れリスクに注意が必要である。 
10 月２日に公表された９月の東京都区部ＣＰＩコアは前年比＋2.7％と、８月の同＋1.8％から
大幅に伸びが拡大した。特殊要因による押し上げの影響も大きいものの、それを除いても伸びは
高まっており、足元で物価上昇圧力が強まっていることが示されている。先行きも、日用品や食
料品を中心とした既往のコスト上昇の価格転嫁や、ＡＩ需要と円安を背景とした耐久財価格の上
昇、政府支援の終了や燃料価格上昇による電気・ガス代の押し上げなどから、物価はこの先伸び
を高め、ＣＰＩコアは26 年末から27 年初にかけて前年比＋３％を上回ることが予想される（消
費者物価指数（東京都区部・2026 年 9 月） ～特殊要因を除いても強い。財を中心に物価上昇圧力
強まる～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所） 
名目賃金は先行きも高い伸びを維持するとみられるが、物価上昇率が年末にかけて再び高まれ
ば、実質賃金の改善はいったん途切れる可能性がある。実質賃金が再びマイナスに転じる可能性
も十分あるだろう。 
このように、実質賃金改善の持続性は引き続き物価次第である。企業部門はＡＩ関連需要や価
格転嫁を背景として、総じて好調さを維持している一方、物価上昇率が再び高まれば、家計の実
質購買力は圧迫される。今後は、企業部門の好調さが続くなかで、家計部門が物価上昇の逆風に
どこまで耐えられるかが、景気の持続性を左右する重要なポイントになるだろう。

4. 注目される 2027 年春闘 
次に注目されるのが、今後の賃上げの持続性を占う2027 年春闘である。2026 年春闘では、連合
ベースの賃上げ率が5.01％と、３年連続で５％台の高い伸びとなった。2027 年春闘では、こうし
た高い賃上げが４年目も続くかが焦点となる。人手不足や採用競争の激化、物価上昇、高水準の
企業収益は引き続き賃上げを後押しする要因であり、現時点では企業の賃上げ姿勢が大きく後退
している様子はみられない。 
もっとも、数年にわたる賃上げで人件費負担が累積しているほか、中東情勢を背景とした原材
料費や物流費の上昇も企業収益の下押し要因となる。こうした環境下でも高い賃上げが維持され
るのか、また、大企業だけでなく中小企業にも賃上げが広がるのかが重要なポイントになる。 
春闘の方向性を判断するための材料は、３月の集中回答日を待たず、今後徐々に明らかにな
る。目先注目されるのが、毎年10 月中旬に公表される連合の「春季生活闘争基本構想」であり、
ここで労働組合側の2027 年春闘に向けたスタンスがある程度確認される。また、11 月上～中旬頃
には経団連の経労委報告原案が判明し、報道されることが多い。ここで経営側のスタンスもある
程度把握することができる。その後も年末にかけて、主要産業別労働組合の要求方針案が明らか
になるほか、早期に賃上げ方針を表明する企業も出てくる。こうした動きを通じて、2027 年春闘

### Page 5

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

に向けた労使双方の姿勢や賃上げの「初動のモメンタム」を確認していくことになる。（2027 年春
闘の注目点については、2027 年春闘のスケジュールと金融政策 ～2027 年春闘の行方は3 月より

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
120 收藏 分享到 :
全美地产经纪商协会公布数据， 9 ⽉份成屋待售合同签约指数骤降 7.3% ⾄ 66 ，创下 2001 年
有记录以来的最低值，降幅超出市场预期。，美国抵押贷款利率⾃伊朗战争爆发以来持续攀
升，⽬前已升⾄近三年来的最⾼⽔平，是此次下滑的主要原因。
摘要：
美国房地产市场深陷僵局，最新数据显⽰其降温程度已触及历史极值。
全美地产经纪商协会（ NAR ）周⼆公布数据显⽰， 9 ⽉份成屋待售合同签约指数骤降 7.3% ⾄
66 ，创下 2001 年有记录以来的最低值，降幅超出彭博调查中所有经济学家的预测。
与此同时，另⼀项单独数据显⽰， 9 ⽉新屋开⼯量放缓⾄ 2020 年以来的最低⽔平之⼀，住房市场
的整体疲态进⼀步加剧。
⾼企的抵押贷款利率是此次下滑的直接推⼿。抵押贷款利率⾃伊朗战争爆发以来持续攀升，⽬

前已升⾄近三年来的最⾼⽔平。 NAR ⾸席经济学家 Lawrence Yun 在声明中表⽰：
全⾯下滑，西部跌幅最深
9 ⽉份成屋待售合同签约量的萎缩波及美国所有地区，呈现全⾯性下⾏态势。其中西部地区降幅
最为显著，达 9.2% ；成屋销售规模最⼤的南部地区跌幅亦达 8.2% 。
以未经季节性调整的数据计算，合同签约量较去年同期下降近 13% ，年度跌幅同样触⽬惊⼼。
成屋待售合同签约数据历来被视为衡量成屋销售的前瞻性指标，因为合同签约通常发⽣在成交
完成前⼀⾄两个⽉。此次指数跌⾄历史低点，意味着未来数⽉内已完成交割的成屋销售数据或
将进⼀步承压。
结合新屋开⼯放缓的数据，当前住房市场的困境在需求与供给两端同步呈现，在⾼借贷成本与
居⾼不下的房价双重压制下，市场的实质性改善仍取决于抵押贷款利率的⾛向。
"9 ⽉市场状况因抵押贷款利率快速攀升⽽持续⾛弱，买卖双⽅可能都在等待利率出现有利转机后
再⾏动。 "

Document 756: 股票市場總評：明年估值有轉機

Source PDF: all report pdf/股票市場總評：明年估值有轉機.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

台北富邦銀行 
October 16，2026 | 9:30 AM (GMT+8) 
股票市場總評： 明年估值有轉機
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎財政債務攀升，QE 壓抑期限溢價 
Fed 於 2026 年發布的研究，運用 DKW 利率期限結構
模型，分析政府債務增加如何影響長期美債殖利率。市
場通常觀察的 10 年期美債殖利率三大組成分別為實質
利率預期、通膨預期、期限溢價。前兩項合計就是市場
對未來短期名目利率的平均預期，反映經濟、通膨與貨
幣政策展望 。 期限溢價則是投資人選擇持有長期債券要
求的額外風險補償。因此，即使Fed 政策利率不變，市
場上修未來利率預期，或要求更高的長債風險補償，都
可能推升 10 年期美債殖利率。

該研究選擇觀察 2021 年喬治亞州參議員決選，當時民
主黨須贏兩席，才能取得參議院控制權，提高額外財政
刺激通過的機會 ， 最終選舉結果使財政刺激的實現機率
大幅提高，市場預期政府赤字因而上修約4,500 億美元。
研究利用這項短時間內發生的財政預期變化 ， 估計政府
債務增加對長期利率的影響。表 1 顯示，預期公共債務
占 GDP 每增加 1 個百分點，10 年期 TIPS 實質殖利率
估計上升 2.58 個基點，經 DKW 模型拆解，預期實質
短率與期限溢價的估計增幅分別為 1.20 及 1.71 個基
點，原始估計分別達 10%及 5%統計顯著水準，顯示政
府債增加對長債風險補償的推升效果更為明確。

表 1 美國政府債務增加對實質利率及期限溢價的影響

資料來源：2026/5/26 Fed 
備註：本表衡量市場預期公共債務占 GDP 每增加 1 個百分點，對各
項利率的影響，單位為基點（bp）。10 Year 為當前 10 年期利率，
10 Year, 5-Years-Forward 為 5 年後起算的 10 年期遠期利率。TIPS
為抗通膨公債實質殖利率，Exp. r*為 DKW 模型估計的預期實質短
率，Real T.P.為實質期限溢價，反映投資人承擔長期實質利率風險所
要求的額外補償。本文正文統一使用 「期限溢價」 ， 以名目口徑討論 。
名目期限溢價概念上包含實質期限溢價及通膨風險溢價 ， 在通膨風險
溢價不變的假設下，可用 Fed 估計的實質期限溢價變動，近似推算
名目期限溢價的變動。One Day 為 2021/1/5 收盤至 1/6 收盤的事
件觀察期間，Bootstrap p-val 數值越小代表統計證據越強，*及**分
別表示達 10%及 5%統計顯著水準。

實質短率的長期水準則可參考中性利率 ，中性利率又稱
自然利率，也就是經濟充分運作、通膨穩定時，既不刺
激也不抑制經濟的實質短期利率 。 自然利率不是直接觀
察到的市場報價，而是模型估計的均衡水準，可作為判 
◼ 期限溢價是投資人持有長債要求的風險補償 ，在目前情境下，若僅考慮期限溢價
上升，預期 10 年美債殖利率最高達 5.6%，若實質短率同步上升 ，則可能升至
5.8%，若殖利率突破 5.8%且由期限溢價帶動，代表不確定性進一步增溫，使市場
要求比過往更高的風險補償，短期美債殖利率維持高檔，令美股本益比承壓。 
◼ 成品油供需有望自今年底逐步改善，EIA 預期美國汽柴油價格回落，東岸餾分油
庫存於明年 Q2 有望改善，能源通膨壓力可望減輕。配合非農就業新增已處歷史
偏低水準，若核心通膨未再加速、就業維持偏弱水準，最快明年下半年仍有預防
性降息機會，有利政策利率預期下修，緩解美股估值壓力，中期展望審慎樂觀。 
◼ 科技股短線承壓，可逢估值低檔布局、波段區間操作。另一方面，市場共識預估
羅素 2000 指數 EPS 增速將由今年 32%加快至明年 50%，與其他主要指數放緩
形成對比，明年稍晚仍可留意降息帶動小型股估值回升及獲利加速的雙重契機。

*：舉例說明，非單一標的推薦 
誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
斷長期資金成本的參考 。 觀察 HLW 模型 ， 儘管美國政
府債務占 GDP 持續攀升，自然利率卻由 1980 年代約
3%~4%降至約 1%（圖 1） ，反映實質利率同時受到人
口結構、生產力及儲蓄投資需求等因素影響，未必隨債
務增加而同步上升。因此，本次以供需傳導較直接、實
證結果較明確的期限溢價作為主要壓力情境 ， 再將預期
實質短率上修納入延伸情境。

圖 1 政府債務攀升，實質自然利率未同步走高

資料來源：2026/10/8 Bloomberg

以 2006 年底 ACM 期限溢價約 0.29%、當時公共債務
占 GDP 約 35.4%為基準（圖 2），至 2025 年底債務
率已升至 99.5%，增加 64.1 個百分點。依 Fed 研究線
性推算，期限溢價的財政調整幅度約 110 個基點，但
10/8 ACM 期限溢價約 0.92%，相較 2006 年底僅增加
約 63 個基點（圖 3）。金融海嘯後債務率普遍高於過
往 ， 期限溢價卻維持相對低檔 ， 可能與Fed 大規模量化
寬鬆 （QE） 吸收長天期債券供給有關，Fed 於 2017 年
的研究估計，2016 年底大規模資產購買使 10 年期期
限溢價降低約 99 個基點，90%信賴區間為降低 52 至
146 個基點。

圖 2 債務壓力上升，將推升期限溢價

資料來源：2026/10/8 Bloomberg

圖 3 QE 壓低期限溢價，與債務比走勢一度脫鉤

資料來源：2026/10/8 Bloomberg

▎債務供給增，長端利率仍有上行壓力 
根據 CBO 基準預測 ，公共債務占 GDP 比重至 2036 年
將升至約 120%（圖 4），相較本次採用的 2025 年底
99.5%基準，增加 20.5 個百分點。本次以 10/8 美債殖
利率 5.23%及 ACM 期限溢價約 0.92%為市場起點 ， 假
設既有財政債務與 Fed 持債效果已反映在當前價格中 ，
再將未來債務增量依 Fed 研究係數納入壓力測試。於
預期通膨、通膨風險補償及央行持債效果不變下，期限
溢價與預期實質短率的累積上修幅度分別約 35 及 25
個基點。此外 ， 科技大廠龐大資本支出使債務融資大增 ，
產生資金排擠壓力，亦增添長端殖利率上行壓力。

圖 4 公共債務占 GDP 比重將進入新一輪上行週期

資料來源：2026/10/8 CBO

依上述估算，僅考慮期限溢價增加，10 年期美債殖利
率對應約 5.6%，若預期實質短率同步上修，則對應約
5.8%。CBO 於 10/8 發布財政赤字估算 ， 其中使用的十
年債殖利率假設為 4.8%及 5.8%，其中較高的 5.8%情
境與本次估算壓力情境相近。因此，5.6%至 5.8%可作
為本次財政壓力測試的參考區間 ，若殖利率持續超過
5.8%， 且主要由期限溢價帶動 ，代表市場不確定性進一
步增溫，使投資人要求高於歷史敏感度推算的風險補償。

▎能源供給衝擊明年有望逐步緩解 
今年能源價格上漲除了受到原油供給中斷影響 ， 成品油
供需緊張亦推升裂解價差 ， 使柴油等燃料價格漲幅進一
步擴大。EIA 於 10/6 發布的展望顯示，隨原油價格回
落及成品油庫存逐步改善 ， 美國汽柴油價格有望自今年
底逐漸下降，並於 2027 年延續回落趨勢（圖 5），顯
示能源價格對通膨的上行壓力可望逐步減輕。

圖 5 美國成品油價格明年有望顯著回落

資料來源：2026/10 EIA

成品油庫存變化亦支持明年供需改善的展望 。EIA 指出 ，
今年 9 月美國東岸柴油等餾分油庫存較過去五年同期
平均低 32%， 顯示目前供給仍相當緊張 。 儘管冬季需求
可能使庫存維持低檔，但預期 2027/4 庫存觸及當年低
點後回補，至 2027 年底缺口縮至約 15%(圖 6)，屆時
將為通膨降溫及 Fed 降低後續緊縮幅度創造有利條件 。

圖 6 美國東岸餾分油庫存缺口明年第二季後有望收斂

資料來源：2026/10 EIA 
備註：圖中藍線為美國東岸餾分油庫存，灰色虛線為2021~2025 年
同期平均。餾分油主要包括柴油及取暖油等成品油，不包含汽油。美
國東岸煉油產能有限，須仰賴美國墨西哥灣沿岸供應及海外進口，因
此對國際成品油供需變化較為敏感 ， 可作為燃料供應緊張程度的指標 。

▎本輪升息就業偏弱，明年稍晚或降息 
目前市場已反應未來一年 Fed 將再升息約 3 碼，已較
9 月點陣圖預估 2027 年底政策利率再升 1 碼更為鷹派，
若通膨未再明顯加速， 市場進一步上修升息幅度的空間
可能有限。

回顧 1990 年後六次升息循環（表 3），以首次升息前
一個月的資料比較 ， 非農新增就業三個月平均介於22.8
萬至 53.2 萬人，位於歷史第 66 至第 98 百分位。本輪
2026/9 升息前，2026/8 非農新增三個月平均僅約 5.1
萬人 ， 位於第22 百分位 ， 不僅低於過去六次升息起點 ，
也明顯低於長期一般水準 。 相較過往在較強勁的就業背
景下開始緊縮 ， 本輪貨幣政策必須更早權衡抑制通膨與
避免就業進一步轉弱的風險。

1990 年後共有六次升降息循環，最後一次升息至首次
降息的等待時間介於 5~18 個月。1994/2~1995/2、
1999/6~2000/5、2015/12~2018/12 三次升息循環 ，
分別在停止升息後 5、8 及 7 個月轉向降息，其餘三次
則等待 14~18 個月 。進一步觀察首次降息的經濟背景 ，
六次案例中有五次的非農新增就業三個月平均低於歷
史第 40 百分位(表 4)， 通膨與消費則未呈現同樣一致的
低檔特徵，顯示就業降溫可能是促使 Fed 調整政策的
重要訊號。以2024/9 首次降息為例，核心PCE 年增率
仍約 2.84%，名目個人消費支出年增率約 5.7%，非農

新增就業三個月平均卻已降至約 7.2 萬人，反映即使通
膨尚未完全回到 2%，消費亦未明顯萎縮，Fed 仍可能
因就業風險升高而預防性降息。

2026/8 非農就業新增人數水準不僅明顯弱於過去升息
起點，也已接近多數歷史降息案例的偏弱水準。若明年
能源供給改善帶動通膨壓力緩和，而就業維持偏弱水準，
明年稍晚仍可留意預防性降息的機會。

▎股市承壓區間操作，中長期審慎樂觀 
綜合而言，美國財政債務持續擴張，且能源價格仍處高
檔，通膨及後續升息預期尚未完全消退，10 年期美債
殖利率仍有進一步上行壓力，加上政策不確定性高，可
能使美股維持高檔震盪，甚至偏下修正。然而，展望明
年，隨能源供給逐步修復、通膨壓力緩和，市場對後續
緊縮的預期有望降溫，高盛亦預期未來數季 10 年期美
債殖利率逐步回落（圖 7）。若明年稍晚市場重新交易
降息預期，將有助帶動估值回升，中長期股市展望仍可
審慎樂觀。股市配置方面 ，AI 投資與應用需求持續擴大 ，
長期獲利成長動能仍佳，可趁大幅修正時布局。此外，
市場共識預估 2027 年羅素 2000 EPS 增速將由今年的
32%加快至 50%(圖 8)，與其他指數獲利增速普遍放緩
形成對比，亦值得提前關注。

圖 7 高盛估年底 10 年美債殖利率將回落

資料來源：2026/10/9 高盛
